Learn English/Parts of speech/04-adjectives/catalog

More adjectives: E–F#

Adjectives E–F beyond the grammar groups — the everyday adjectives that a triage of WordNet’s adjective lemmas showed a learner needs.

← The grammar of adjectives · All groups

Most English adjectives behave: they go before a noun or after be, and they compare with -er or more. These are those — but each still says how it compares, where it can stand, and what it takes. WordNet lists about twenty thousand adjectives; most of the rest are technical, archaic or British-only, and a triage pass left them out on purpose.

eagle-eyed#

Pronunciation
/ˌiːɡəlˈaɪd/ · ee-guhl-YD
Русский
зоркий, внимательный
Type
compound
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more eagle-eyed, most eagle-eyed
Form
compound — noun + eyed

Very good at seeing or noticing small details.

  1. An eagle-eyed reader spotted the typo on page one.
  2. The goalie was eagle-eyed all game.
  3. The most eagle-eyed fans noticed the hidden clue in the trailer.

earnest#

Pronunciation
/ˈɜːrnɪst/ · UR-nist
Русский
серьёзный, искренний
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more earnest, most earnest
Contrast
in earnest is a phrase: The work began in earnest (seriously, fully); Are you in earnest? (Do you really mean it?).

Very serious and sincere, sometimes too serious.

  1. He's an earnest young man who wants to help.
  2. They had a long, earnest conversation about the future.
  3. Her second speech was more earnest and less funny.

earthen#

Pronunciation
/ˈɜːrθən/ · UR-thuhn
Русский
земляной; глиняный
Type
material
Position
attributive only
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-en from earth

Made of earth or baked clay.

  1. The old cabin had an earthen floor.
  2. Engineers inspected the earthen dam after the flood.
  3. Water was kept cool in large earthen jars.

earthly#

Pronunciation
/ˈɜːrθli/ · URTH-lee
Русский
земной; (после no/any) какой-либо
Type
classifying; also emphasizing
Position
attributive only
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ly from earth
Opposite
heavenly

Earthly has 2 distinct senses.

1. Relating to life in this world, not to heaven or the spirit. (земной, мирской)

  1. He gave away all his earthly possessions.
  2. Their earthly life was hard, but their faith was strong.

2. After no, any or what: at all, possible. (какой бы то ни было, никакой)

  1. There's no earthly reason to wake up at five.
  2. What earthly use is a phone without a battery?

earthy#

Pronunciation
/ˈɜːrθi/ · UR-thee
Русский
землистый; простой, грубоватый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — earthier, earthiest
Form
-y from earth

Earthy has 2 distinct senses.

1. Tasting, smelling or looking like soil. (землистый, с запахом земли)

  1. Mushrooms have an earthy flavor.
  2. The room was decorated in warm, earthy colors.
  3. This coffee is earthier than the Colombian one.

2. Direct and natural, and not shy about the body or sex. (простой, приземлённый, грубоватый)

  1. She has an earthy sense of humor.
  2. His grandmother was a warm, earthy woman who laughed a lot.

east#

Pronunciation
/iːst/ · EEST
Русский
восточный
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only
Comparison
not gradable
Opposite
west
Contrast
In drive east it is an adverb, and in the East a noun. east is mostly for fixed names and parts: the East Coast, the east side; eastern is the general word: eastern Europe, eastern states.

East has 2 distinct senses.

1. In, on or facing the east. (восточный)

  1. They live on the east side of town.
  2. Take the east exit from the parking garage.
  3. We moved to the East Coast last year.

2. Of wind: blowing from the east. (восточный (о ветре))

  1. A cold east wind blew all day.
  2. The east winds brought rain from the ocean.

East African#

Pronunciation
/ˌiːst ˈæfrɪkən/ · eest AF-ri-kuhn
Русский
восточноафриканский
Type
origin
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — east + African

Of or from eastern Africa, such as Kenya, Tanzania, Ethiopia or Uganda.

  1. East African runners dominate long-distance races.
  2. The restaurant serves East African food.
  3. Swahili is spoken in many East African countries.

East German#

Pronunciation
/ˌiːst ˈdʒɜːrmən/ · eest JUR-muhn
Русский
восточногерманский, гэдээровский
Type
origin
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — east + German
Contrast
Historical: the German Democratic Republic existed from 1949 to 1990. For the region today, say eastern German or in eastern Germany.

Of or from the former German Democratic Republic.

  1. His parents escaped from East German territory in 1961.
  2. East German athletes won many Olympic medals.
  3. The car she drove was East German.

eastbound#

Pronunciation
/ˈiːstbaʊnd/ · EEST-bownd
Русский
идущий на восток
Type
classifying
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — east + -bound
Opposite
westbound

Traveling or leading toward the east.

  1. There's a crash in the eastbound lanes of I-90.
  2. The eastbound train leaves at 7:15.
  3. Is this platform eastbound or westbound?

easterly#

Pronunciation
/ˈiːstərli/ · EES-tur-lee
Русский
восточный (о направлении, ветре)
Type
classifying
Position
both — mostly before a noun: an easterly wind
Comparison
not gradable — but most easterly means 'farthest east'
Form
-ly from east
Opposite
westerly
Contrast
An easterly wind blows from the east, but an easterly direction is toward the east.

Easterly has 2 distinct senses.

1. Toward or in the east. (восточный, в восточном направлении)

  1. The ship sailed in an easterly direction.
  2. Maine is the most easterly state in the country.

2. Of wind: blowing from the east. (восточный (о ветре))

  1. Strong easterly winds kept the boats in the harbor.
  2. The forecast calls for light easterly breezes.

Eastern Orthodox#

Pronunciation
/ˌiːstərn ˈɔːrθədɑːks/ · EES-turn OR-thuh-dahks
Русский
православный
Type
origin
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — Eastern + Orthodox
Contrast
In American English the Russian Church is usually called Russian Orthodox; Eastern Orthodox names the whole family of Orthodox churches. orthodox with a small o means 'traditional, accepted': orthodox methods.

Of the Orthodox Christian churches of Eastern Europe, Greece and the Middle East.

  1. Most of her family is Eastern Orthodox.
  2. Eastern Orthodox Christians often celebrate Easter on a different date.
  3. They were married in an Eastern Orthodox church in Chicago.

easternmost#

Pronunciation
/ˈiːstərnmoʊst/ · EES-turn-mohst
Русский
самый восточный
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only
Comparison
not gradable — it is already a superlative
Form
eastern + -most
Opposite
westernmost

Farthest to the east.

  1. Lubec, Maine, is the easternmost town in the US.
  2. We visited the easternmost point of the island.
  3. The easternmost part of the state is mostly farmland.

eastward#

Pronunciation
/ˈiːstwərd/ · EEST-wurd
Русский
направленный на восток
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only
Comparison
not gradable
Form
east + -ward
Opposite
westward
Contrast
After a verb it is an adverb: The storm moved eastward. eastwards with an s is more British.

Moving or facing toward the east.

  1. The storm continued its eastward path.
  2. Our eastward flight took only four hours.
  3. The house has an eastward view of the mountains.

easygoing#

Pronunciation
/ˌiːziˈɡoʊɪŋ/ · ee-zee-GOH-ing
Русский
покладистый, спокойный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more easygoing, most easygoing
Form
compound — easy + going; one word in American spelling
Opposite
uptight

Relaxed and calm, not easily upset or worried.

  1. My new roommate is really easygoing.
  2. He has an easygoing attitude about money.
  3. She's much more easygoing than her sister.

ebullient#

Pronunciation
/ɪˈbʊljənt/ · ih-BUUL-yuhnt
Русский
восторженный, кипучий
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more ebullient, most ebullient

Very cheerful, excited and full of energy.

  1. The coach was in an ebullient mood after the win.
  2. She's an ebullient host who makes everyone feel welcome.
  3. He was even more ebullient than usual at the reunion.

eccentric#

Pronunciation
/ɪkˈsɛntrɪk/ · ik-SEN-trik
Русский
эксцентричный, чудаковатый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more eccentric, most eccentric
Contrast
an eccentric is also a noun: He's a lovable eccentric.

Behaving or looking strange and unusual, often in a harmless or amusing way.

  1. My eccentric uncle keeps goats in his backyard.
  2. Her fashion sense is a little eccentric.
  3. He got more eccentric as he got older.

eclectic#

Pronunciation
/ɪˈklɛktɪk/ · ih-KLEK-tik
Русский
разнообразный, эклектичный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more eclectic, most eclectic

Made up of many different styles, types or ideas.

  1. He has eclectic taste in music, from jazz to heavy metal.
  2. The café is decorated in an eclectic mix of styles.
  3. This year's lineup is more eclectic than ever.

eco-friendly#

Pronunciation
/ˌiːkoʊˈfrɛndli/ · ee-koh-FREND-lee
Русский
экологичный, экологически чистый
Type
compound — eco- + friendly
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more eco-friendly, most eco-friendly
Form
compound — eco- + friendly, like user-friendly
Contrast
friendly here means not harmful to, not kind. Environmentally friendly is the more formal version; green is the everyday one.

Not harmful to the environment.

  1. We switched to eco-friendly cleaning products.
  2. Bamboo is more eco-friendly than plastic.
  3. The new office was designed to be eco-friendly.

ecological#

Pronunciation
/ˌiːkəˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/ · ee-kuh-LAH-ji-kuhl
Русский
экологический
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ical from ecology
Contrast
False friend: Russian экологичный or экологически чистый is eco-friendly, green or environmentally friendly in English: eco-friendly packaging, not ecological packaging. ecological means 'relating to nature and the environment': an ecological disaster.

Relating to the relationships between living things and their environment.

  1. The oil spill was an ecological disaster.
  2. Scientists are studying the ecological impact of the new dam.
  3. Wetlands have enormous ecological value.

Ecuadorian#

Pronunciation
/ˌɛkwəˈdɔːriən/ · ek-wuh-DOR-ee-uhn
Русский
эквадорский
Type
origin
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ian from Ecuador

Of or from Ecuador, its people or its culture.

  1. We tried Ecuadorian ceviche in Queens.
  2. Her father is Ecuadorian and her mother is Peruvian.
  3. The Ecuadorian coast is famous for its bananas and shrimp.

ecumenical#

Pronunciation
/ˌɛkjəˈmɛnɪkəl/ · ek-yuh-MEN-i-kuhl
Русский
экуменический; разносторонний
Type
classifying
Position
both — the religious sense mostly before a noun: an ecumenical service
Comparison
not gradable — but more ecumenical in the 'broad' sense

Ecumenical has 2 distinct senses.

1. Bringing together different Christian churches or different religions. (экуменический, межконфессиональный)

  1. The town held an ecumenical prayer service after the tragedy.
  2. Leaders from several churches joined the ecumenical council.

2. Including or accepting many different kinds of things. (разносторонний, всеохватный)

  1. The festival takes an ecumenical approach to music.
  2. Her reading habits are wonderfully ecumenical.

edgy#

Pronunciation
/ˈɛdʒi/ · EJ-ee
Русский
нервный; дерзкий, провокационный
Type
quality; also emotion
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — edgier, edgiest
Form
-y from edge

Edgy has 2 distinct senses.

1. Nervous, tense and easily annoyed. (нервный, взвинченный)

  1. Everyone was edgy while we waited for the results.
  2. Too much coffee makes me edgy.

2. Daring, modern and a little shocking. (дерзкий, смелый, провокационный)

  1. The comedian is known for his edgy jokes.
  2. The band's new album is edgier than their last one.

edible#

Pronunciation
/ˈɛdəbəl/ · ED-uh-buhl
Русский
съедобный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable — but barely edible, more edible when it means 'good enough to eat'
Opposite
inedible
Contrast
edibles is a plural noun, in American English often 'food containing cannabis'.

Edible has 2 distinct senses.

1. Safe to eat. (съедобный)

  1. Not all wild mushrooms are edible.
  2. The flowers on the cake are edible.
  3. We learned to find edible plants in the woods.

2. Good enough to eat, though not very good. (съедобный, есть можно)

  1. The cafeteria food was barely edible.
  2. With some salt, the soup was at least edible.

editorial#

Pronunciation
/ˌɛdɪˈtɔːriəl/ · ed-i-TOR-ee-uhl
Русский
редакционный, редакторский
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ial from editor
Contrast
an editorial is a noun: an article that gives a newspaper's opinion.

Editorial has 2 distinct senses.

1. Relating to editing or to the people who prepare a book, newspaper or magazine. (редакционный, редакторский)

  1. She joined the magazine's editorial staff.
  2. The paper insists on its editorial independence.

2. Giving an opinion rather than just reporting facts. (редакционный, выражающий мнение)

  1. The editorial page supported the mayor.
  2. He draws editorial cartoons for a local paper.

educated#

Pronunciation
/ˈɛdʒəkeɪtɪd/ · EJ-uh-kay-tid
Русский
образованный; обоснованный
Type
participial
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more educated, most educated; also better educated, well-educated
Form
-ed from educate
Opposite
uneducated

Educated has 2 distinct senses.

1. Having had a good education and knowing a lot. (образованный)

  1. She's an educated woman who speaks three languages.
  2. Voters in the suburbs tend to be more educated.
  3. He was educated at a small college in Ohio.

2. Of a guess or decision: based on some knowledge, so likely to be right. (обоснованный, основанный на знании)

  1. I don't know the exact number, but I can make an educated guess.
  2. Read the reviews so you can make an educated choice.

educational#

Pronunciation
/ˌɛdʒəˈkeɪʃənəl/ · ej-uh-KAY-shuh-nuhl
Русский
образовательный; познавательный
Type
classifying; also quality
Position
both — the 'relating to education' sense only before a noun: the educational system
Comparison
more/most — more educational, most educational, in the 'teaches you something' sense
Form
-al from education

Educational has 2 distinct senses.

1. Relating to education and schools. (образовательный, учебный)

  1. The educational system needs more funding.
  2. He works for an educational nonprofit.

2. Teaching you something useful or interesting. (познавательный, поучительный)

  1. The trip to the museum was really educational.
  2. We try to buy educational toys for the kids.
  3. The documentary was more educational than the textbook.

Edwardian#

Pronunciation
/ɛdˈwɔːrdiən/ · ed-WOR-dee-uhn
Русский
эдвардианский
Type
classifying
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ian from Edward

From the time of King Edward VII of Britain, 1901 to 1910, or in its style.

  1. They restored a beautiful Edwardian house.
  2. The movie is set in Edwardian England.
  3. The costumes in the play were Edwardian.

eerie#

Pronunciation
/ˈɪri/ · IR-ee
Русский
жуткий, зловещий
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — eerier, eeriest

Strange and frightening in a mysterious way.

  1. An eerie silence fell over the empty school.
  2. It was eerie how much he looked like his late father.
  3. The abandoned mall is eerier at night.

effeminate#

Pronunciation
/ɪˈfɛmɪnət/ · ih-FEM-i-nuht
Русский
женоподобный, изнеженный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more effeminate, most effeminate
Contrast
Used about men and usually disapproving or offensive. It is not a neutral word for feminine.

Of a man: looking or behaving in a way thought to be typical of women.

  1. He was bullied at school for being effeminate.
  2. Critics called the singer's style effeminate.
  3. In the remake, the character is less effeminate and more serious.

effervescent#

Pronunciation
/ˌɛfərˈvɛsənt/ · ef-ur-VES-uhnt
Русский
шипучий; искрящийся, жизнерадостный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more effervescent, most effervescent

Effervescent has 2 distinct senses.

1. Of a person: lively, cheerful and full of energy. (искрящийся, жизнерадостный)

  1. She has an effervescent personality.
  2. The most effervescent guest at the party was my grandmother.

2. Of a liquid: producing small bubbles of gas. (шипучий, газированный)

  1. Drop the effervescent tablet into a glass of water.
  2. The wine is light and slightly effervescent.

effete#

Pronunciation
/ɪˈfiːt/ · ih-FEET
Русский
изнеженный, слабый, выродившийся
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more effete, most effete
Contrast
A disapproving, bookish word; in American political talk it often mocks educated elites: effete snobs.

Weak, too refined and lacking energy or strength.

  1. The novel mocks the effete aristocrats of old Europe.
  2. He dismissed his critics as effete intellectuals.
  3. The new version sounds more effete than the original.

effortless#

Pronunciation
/ˈɛfərtləs/ · EF-urt-luhs
Русский
лёгкий, непринуждённый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more effortless, most effortless
Form
-less from effort

Done so well that it seems to need no effort.

  1. She made the difficult dance look effortless.
  2. He has an effortless style of writing.
  3. Her piano playing grew more effortless every year.

effusive#

Pronunciation
/ɪˈfjuːsɪv/ · ih-FYOO-siv
Русский
восторженный, бурный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more effusive, most effusive
Takes
in + noun: effusive in her praise

Showing strong feelings of praise, thanks or welcome, sometimes too much.

  1. The reviews of the play were effusive.
  2. He gave me an effusive welcome at the door.
  3. She was more effusive in her thanks than I expected.

egalitarian#

Pronunciation
/ɪˌɡælɪˈtɛriən/ · ih-gal-i-TAIR-ee-uhn
Русский
эгалитарный, основанный на равенстве
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more egalitarian, most egalitarian

Believing that all people are equal and should have the same rights and chances.

  1. They share the housework in a very egalitarian way.
  2. The company has an egalitarian culture with no private offices.
  3. The Nordic countries are among the most egalitarian in the world.

egocentric#

Pronunciation
/ˌiːɡoʊˈsɛntrɪk/ · ee-goh-SEN-trik
Русский
эгоцентричный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more egocentric, most egocentric
Form
compound — ego + centric

Thinking only about yourself and not about other people.

  1. Toddlers are naturally egocentric.
  2. He's an egocentric boss who never listens.
  3. She became more egocentric after she got famous.

egotistical#

Pronunciation
/ˌiːɡəˈtɪstɪkəl/ · ee-guh-TIS-ti-kuhl
Русский
самовлюблённый, эгоистичный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more egotistical, most egotistical
Form
-ical from egotist
Opposite
modest
Contrast
egotistical means 'thinking you are very important and talking about yourself'; selfish means 'caring only about your own needs'. Russian эгоистичный is usually selfish.

Thinking you are better or more important than other people.

  1. He's so egotistical that he only talks about himself.
  2. The egotistical star refused to share the stage.
  3. She's the most egotistical person I've ever worked with.

egregious#

Pronunciation
/ɪˈɡriːdʒəs/ · ih-GREE-juhs
Русский
вопиющий, возмутительный
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more egregious, most egregious

Extremely bad and very obvious.

  1. It was an egregious error by the referee.
  2. The company was fined for egregious safety violations.
  3. This is the most egregious case of fraud the court has seen.

Egyptian#

Pronunciation
/ɪˈdʒɪpʃən/ · ih-JIP-shuhn
Русский
египетский
Type
origin
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ian from Egypt

Of or from Egypt, ancient or modern, its people or its culture.

  1. The museum has a large collection of ancient Egyptian art.
  2. My doctor is Egyptian.
  3. Egyptian cotton sheets are soft and expensive.

elaborate#

Pronunciation
/ɪˈlæbərət/ · ih-LAB-ur-uht
Русский
сложный, продуманный, замысловатый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more elaborate, most elaborate
Opposite
simple
Contrast
The adjective ends in /ərət/; the verb to elaborate (give more details) ends in /əreɪt/: Can you ih-LAB-uh-rayt?

Having many carefully planned parts or details.

  1. They planned an elaborate surprise party.
  2. The dress had elaborate embroidery on the sleeves.
  3. Each year the holiday decorations get more elaborate.

elastic#

Pronunciation
/ɪˈlæstɪk/ · ih-LAS-tik
Русский
эластичный, упругий; гибкий
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more elastic, most elastic
Contrast
elastic is also a noun, the stretchy material: The elastic in these shorts is worn out.

Elastic has 2 distinct senses.

1. Able to stretch and then go back to its original shape. (эластичный, упругий, растяжимый)

  1. The pants have an elastic waistband.
  2. Skin becomes less elastic with age.
  3. This fabric is more elastic than cotton.

2. Able to change or be changed easily. (гибкий, растяжимый (о понятии, правилах))

  1. "Soon" is a very elastic word in this office.
  2. Demand for gasoline is not very elastic.

elated#

Pronunciation
/ɪˈleɪtɪd/ · ih-LAY-tid
Русский
ликующий, в восторге
Type
emotion; also participial
Position
both — mostly after a verb: We were elated
Comparison
more/most — more elated, most elated
Takes
at, by or about + noun; to-infinitive; that-clause

Extremely happy and excited.

  1. She was elated at the news of her promotion.
  2. The elated fans ran onto the field.
  3. I've never seen him more elated than on the day his son was born.

elected#

Pronunciation
/ɪˈlɛktɪd/ · ih-LEK-tid
Русский
избранный, выборный
Type
participial
Position
attributive only — after a verb it is the passive: She was elected mayor
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ed from elect
Contrast
A person who has won but not yet started the job is -elect, after the noun: the president-elect, the governor-elect.

Chosen by voting.

  1. Elected officials should listen to voters.
  2. The city has an elected school board.
  3. She was the first elected female governor of the state.

elective#

Pronunciation
/ɪˈlɛktɪv/ · ih-LEK-tiv
Русский
выборный; факультативный; плановый
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ive from elect
Contrast
In American schools an elective is a noun, 'an optional class': I'm taking photography as an elective.

Elective has 2 distinct senses.

1. Chosen, not required; of medical treatment, planned rather than urgent. (факультативный, по выбору; плановый (об операции))

  1. Hospitals postponed all elective surgery.
  2. Art is an elective course at our high school.
  3. Many insurers don't cover elective procedures.

2. Relating to an election or filled by an election. (выборный)

  1. She has never held elective office.
  2. The governor is the highest elective position in the state.

electronic#

Pronunciation
/ɪˌlɛkˈtrɑːnɪk/ · ih-lek-TRAH-nik
Русский
электронный
Type
classifying
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ic from electron
Contrast
electronic devices use microchips and circuits (a phone, a computer); electric things simply run on electricity (an electric fan, an electric car); electrical describes the system or work (electrical wiring, an electrical engineer).

Electronic has 2 distinct senses.

1. Using microchips, transistors or other parts that control electric current. (электронный)

  1. Please turn off all electronic devices during takeoff.
  2. The store sells electronic equipment and appliances.

2. Done, sent or stored using computers. (электронный, цифровой)

  1. We accept electronic payments only.
  2. Doctors now keep electronic medical records.
  3. The music is fully electronic, with no live instruments.

Elegant has 2 distinct senses.

1. Graceful, attractive and stylish. (элегантный, изящный)

  1. She wore an elegant black dress.
  2. The hotel lobby is simple but elegant.
  3. The new design is more elegant than the old one.

2. Of an idea or solution: clever and simple. (изящный, остроумный (о решении))

  1. It's an elegant solution to a difficult problem.
  2. The proof is short and elegant.

elemental#

Pronunciation
/ˌɛlɪˈmɛntəl/ · el-i-MEN-tuhl
Русский
стихийный, первобытный; элементарный (в химии)
Type
quality; also classifying
Position
both — the chemistry sense only before a noun: elemental carbon
Comparison
more/most — more elemental, most elemental
Contrast
Not the same as elementary (basic, simple): an elementary mistake, but elemental forces of nature.

Elemental has 2 distinct senses.

1. Basic and powerful, like the forces of nature. (стихийный, первобытный, глубинный)

  1. The storm showed the elemental power of the sea.
  2. Fear of the dark is one of the most elemental human fears.

2. In chemistry: in the form of a pure chemical element. (элементарный, в чистом виде (о веществе))

  1. Diamonds are made of elemental carbon.
  2. Elemental sodium reacts violently with water.

elliptical#

Elliptical has 2 distinct senses.

  2. They bought an elliptical dining table for the narrow room.

2. Of speech or writing, leaving out words or details, so that it is hard to understand. (недосказанный, туманный)

  1. His answer was short and elliptical.
  2. Her later poems are even more elliptical than her early ones.

embedded#

Pronunciation
/ɪmˈbɛdɪd/ · im-BED-id
Русский

Embedded has 3 distinct senses.

1. Fixed firmly inside something solid. (вмурованный, застрявший)

  1. The doctor removed a piece of glass embedded in his foot.
  2. Fossils are embedded in the rock.

2. Forming a deep, lasting part of a culture, mind or group. (укоренившийся)

  1. Racism was deeply embedded in the town's history.
  2. These habits are so embedded that they are hard to change.

3. Built into a larger system or device, or placed inside a group to work with it. (встроенный; прикомандированный)

  1. The car's embedded software controls the brakes.
  2. You can play the embedded video right on the page.
  3. An embedded reporter traveled with the unit.

emotional#

Pronunciation
/ɪˈmoʊʃənəl/ · ih-MOH-shuh-nuhl

Emotional has 3 distinct senses.

1. Relating to feelings. (эмоциональный, душевный)

  1. Her friends gave her a lot of emotional support.
  2. The divorce took an emotional toll on the kids.

2. Showing strong feelings, or easily showing them. (эмоциональный, взволнованный)

  1. He got very emotional when he talked about his mother.
  2. I'm more emotional than my husband.

3. Causing strong feelings. (трогательный, волнующий)

  1. It was an emotional reunion at the airport.
  2. Abortion is a highly emotional issue.

Enclosed has 2 distinct senses.

Endemic has 2 distinct senses.

1. Of a disease or problem, regularly found in a particular place or group. (распространённый, характерный)

  1. Malaria is endemic in parts of Africa.
  2. Corruption was endemic in the city government.
  3. Health officials say the virus has become endemic.

2. Of a plant or animal, found naturally only in one area. (эндемичный)

  1. The Hawaiian goose is endemic to Hawaii.
  2. The island has dozens of endemic bird species.

Entitled has 3 distinct senses.

2. Believing that you deserve special treatment without earning it. (избалованный, считающий, что ему все должны)

  1. The customers were rude and entitled.

  1. She wrote a memoir entitled "Coming Home."
  2. The report, entitled "Hunger in America," came out in May.

  1. Both genetic and environmental factors affect weight.
  2. Doctors think the cause is environmental.

Epic has 3 distinct senses.

  1. That concert was epic!

Eurasian#

Pronunciation
/jʊˈreɪʒən/ · yuu-RAY-zhuhn
Русский
евразийский; европейско-азиатского происхождения
Type
origin
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable

Eurasian has 2 distinct senses.

1. Of Europe and Asia taken together. (евразийский)

  1. The Eurasian land mass runs from Portugal to Japan.
  2. Eurasian trade routes crossed these mountains.

2. Of mixed European and Asian descent. (имеющий европейско-азиатские корни)

  1. Her mother is Eurasian.
  2. The port city had a large Eurasian community.

Capitalized as a noun for the movement: Evangelicals voted in large numbers.

Evangelical has 2 distinct senses.

  1. He is evangelical about cold showers.
  2. The company has an almost evangelical faith in its own product.

Evasive has 2 distinct senses.

even#

Pronunciation
/ˈiːvən/ · EE-vuhn
Русский
ровный; равный; чётный; спокойный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more even, most even (the 'level' and 'steady' senses)
Opposite
uneven; odd (of numbers)
Contrast
Even is also a very common adverb: even better, even if. That is not this adjective.

Even has 4 distinct senses.

1. Flat, level and smooth. (ровный, гладкий)

  1. Find an even surface for the tripod.
  2. The new concrete is much more even than the old walkway.

2. Equal in number, amount or score, so that nobody owes anything. (равный, поровну)

  1. Split the check into even shares.
  2. You bought lunch, so now we're even.

3. Of a number, able to be divided by two. (чётный)

  1. Houses with even numbers are on this side of the street.
  2. Two, four and six are even numbers.

4. Steady and calm, without sudden changes. (спокойный, ровный)

  1. She answered in an even voice.
  2. Keep the oven at an even temperature.

Evergreen has 2 distinct senses.

  1. "How to save for retirement" is evergreen content.
  2. It's an evergreen question in American politics.

evolutionary#

Pronunciation

Comparison

Form

Contrast
In business writing it means gradual, the opposite of revolutionary.

Evolutionary has 2 distinct senses.

1. Having to do with the slow development of living things over time. (эволюционный)

  1. She teaches evolutionary biology at Michigan.
  2. Color vision gave early primates an evolutionary advantage.

2. Happening by small steps rather than all at once. (постепенный, эволюционный)

  1. The new phone is evolutionary, not revolutionary.
  2. They favor an evolutionary approach to reform.

exclusive#

Pronunciation
/ɪkˈskluːsɪv/ · ik-SKLOO-siv

An exclusive is also a noun in journalism — a story only one outlet has.

Exclusive has 3 distinct senses.

1. Available to one person or group and to nobody else. (эксклюзивный, исключительный)

  1. The magazine has an exclusive interview with the director.
  2. This offer is exclusive to members.

2. Expensive, and open only to people the owners choose. (элитный, закрытый)

  1. They joined an exclusive country club.
  2. It's the most exclusive address in the city.

3. Unable to be true or to happen at the same time as something else. (взаимоисключающий)

  1. The two goals are not mutually exclusive.
  2. These categories are exclusive, so pick only one.

Type
participial
Position
both
Comparison

Making you feel very happy, awake and full of energy.

  1. The ride down the mountain was exhilarating.
  2. There's something exhilarating about a cold morning swim.
  3. Her first week in New York was the most exhilarating of her life.

existential#

Pronunciation
/ˌɛɡzɪˈstɛnʃəl/ · eg-zi-STEN-shuhl
Русский
экзистенциальный; угрожающий самому существованию
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — before a noun: an existential threat
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-al from existence

Existential has 2 distinct senses.

1. Threatening the very existence of something. (угрожающий самому существованию)

  1. Climate change is an existential threat to the islands.
  2. The lawsuit is an existential risk for the company.

2. Having to do with the meaning and purpose of human life. (экзистенциальный)

  1. He's having an existential crisis at forty.
  2. The novel asks existential questions about free will.

existing#

Pronunciation
/ɪɡˈzɪstɪŋ/ · ig-ZIS-ting
Русский

Type
participial
Position
attributive only — before a noun: the existing rules
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ing from exist
Contrast
Use existing for what is already there, existent almost never — the common word is its opposite, nonexistent.

Present now, before any change is made.

  1. The existing law already covers this.
  2. We'll connect the new wing to the existing building.
  3. Existing customers keep their old rate.

Exotic has 2 distinct senses.

  1. The market sells exotic fruit you can't find in stores.

  2. The most exotic thing on the menu was the octopus.

Expansive has 2 distinct senses.

expedient#

Pronunciation

Position

Comparison

Contrast
Not "fast." Expedient means useful right now, often at the cost of what is right — that is expeditious.

Useful or convenient in a situation, though perhaps not honest or fair.

  1. It was politically expedient to blame the previous administration.
  2. They took the expedient route and settled out of court.
  3. Firing him would be more expedient than fixing the process.

explosive#

Pronunciation
/ɪkˈsploʊsɪv/ · ik-SPLOH-siv
Русский
взрывчатый; взрывной, вспыльчивый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more explosive, most explosive
Form
-ive from explode
Contrast
Explosives is also a plural noun — the material itself.

Explosive has 3 distinct senses.

1. Able to blow up. (взрывчатый)

  1. Dogs are trained to sniff out explosive material.
  2. The gas is highly explosive.

2. Likely to cause sudden anger, argument or trouble. (взрывоопасный, скандальный)

  1. The testimony was explosive.
  2. Race and money are the most explosive topics on the council.

3. Sudden, fast and powerful. (взрывной, стремительный)

  1. The company saw explosive growth in its first year.
  2. He's an explosive runner off the line.

express#

Pronunciation
/ɪkˈsprɛs/ · ik-SPRES
Русский
срочный, экспресс-; ясно выраженный
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — before a noun: an express train
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Express is also a common verb (express an opinion) and a noun (take the express). The adjective only goes before a noun.

Express has 2 distinct senses.

1. Fast, and stopping at few places or handled ahead of the rest. (срочный, экспресс-)

  1. Take the express train to Penn Station.
  2. Ten items or fewer in the express lane.
  3. Send it express mail if it has to be there Monday.

2. Stated clearly and on purpose. (ясно выраженный, прямой)

  1. He came for the express purpose of apologizing.
  2. You need her express permission in writing.

-ing from extenuate
Contrast
It lives in one phrase: extenuating circumstances. You will rarely meet it anywhere else.

Making an offense seem less serious by explaining why it happened.

  1. The judge found extenuating circumstances and reduced the sentence.
  2. We can waive the fee if there are extenuating circumstances.
  3. There were extenuating factors the report never mentioned.

Exterior is the outside surface of a thing (a house, a car); external is the wider word for anything outside (external pressure, external hard drive).

On or forming the outside of something, especially a building.

  1. The exterior walls need paint.
  2. Use exterior latex for the trim.
  3. They shot the exterior scenes in Toronto.

External has 2 distinct senses.

Contrast
Extinct (died out) and extinguished (put out, of a fire) come from the same root but are not interchangeable.

Extinct has 2 distinct senses.

1. No longer existing, of a species or a way of life. (вымерший, исчезнувший)

  1. The passenger pigeon has been extinct since 1914.
  2. Several frog species have gone extinct in the last decade.
  3. Pay phones are all but extinct.

2. Of a volcano, no longer active and not expected to erupt again. (потухший)

  1. The island sits on an extinct volcano.
  2. Geologists say the crater is extinct, not dormant.

Americans use Fahrenheit for weather, cooking and body temperature: 32°F is freezing and 98.6°F is normal body temperature. Always with a capital F.

failing#

Pronunciation
/ˈfeɪlɪŋ/ · FAY-ling
Русский
слабеющий; терпящий неудачу; неудовлетворительный

A failing is also a noun, meaning a weakness: We all have our failings.

Failing has 3 distinct senses.

  1. She moved in with her daughter because of failing health.
  2. In the failing light we couldn't see the trail.

2. Not successful; losing money, customers or support. (терпящий неудачу, убыточный)

  1. He took over a failing restaurant and turned it around.

  1. He got a failing grade in chemistry.
  2. Anything below 60 counts as a failing score.

faint#

Pronunciation
/feɪnt/ · FAYNT
Русский
слабый, едва заметный; близкий к обмороку
Type
quality
Position
both — in the 'about to pass out' sense only after a verb: I feel faint
Comparison
-er/-est — fainter, faintest
Contrast
I don't have the faintest idea means 'I have no idea at all'. Faint is also a verb, 'to lose consciousness' (She fainted). Feint (a fake move) sounds the same.

Faint has 3 distinct senses.

1. Hard to see, hear or smell; not strong. (слабый, едва различимый)

  1. There was a faint smell of smoke in the hall.
  2. The music grew fainter as we walked away.
  3. I could see a faint light in the distance.

2. Very small; slight. (слабый, малейший)

  1. There's still a faint hope that they'll find survivors.
  2. I don't have the faintest idea where he went.
  3. She gave a faint smile.

3. Feeling weak and dizzy, as if you are about to lose consciousness. (близкий к обмороку)

  1. I felt faint from the heat.
  2. She was faint with hunger after the long hike.

fair#

Pronunciation
/fɛr/ · FAIR
Русский
справедливый; разумный; светлый; ясный
Type
evaluative; also color
Position
both — in the 'fairly large' sense only before a noun: a fair number
Comparison
-er/-est — fairer, fairest
Takes
to + person: It isn't fair to her; to-infinitive in it's fair to say
Opposite
unfair
Contrast
Russian светлый about hair and skin is fair in a slightly formal style; everyday American English more often says blond hair and light or pale skin. A fair is a noun (the county fair), and in play fair the word is an adverb.

Fair has 6 distinct senses.

1. Treating people equally and according to the rules; just. (справедливый, честный)

  1. It's not fair that she gets more money for the same job.
  2. The judge was strict but fair.
  3. Everyone deserves a fair trial.

2. Reasonable and acceptable in the circumstances. (разумный, приемлемый)

  1. We got a fair price for the car.
  2. That's a fair question.
  3. It's fair to say the plan didn't work.

3. Fairly large in amount, number or distance. (изрядный, немалый)

  1. It's a fair distance from here to the lake.
  2. A fair number of people left early.

4. Neither very good nor very bad; average. (удовлетворительный, средний)

  1. His condition is only fair after the surgery.
  2. The car is in fair shape for its age.

5. Of hair or skin: light in color. (светлый)

  1. People with fair skin burn easily.
  2. She has fairer hair than her sister.
  3. The baby had fair curls.

6. Of weather: pleasant and dry, without rain. (ясный, хороший (о погоде))

  1. The forecast is fair and sunny for the weekend.
  2. We sailed out under fair skies.

fair-haired#

Pronunciation

faithful#

Contrast
About a husband, wife or partner, faithful means not having sex with anyone else, and unfaithful means cheating.

fake#

Pronunciation
/feɪk/ · FAYK
Русский
поддельный, фальшивый; притворный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable — but about insincere people so fake and more fake are common in speech
Opposite
real; genuine
Contrast
Fake vs false: fake is for copies made to fool people (fake money, a fake ID); false is for things that are not true (a false statement) and a few replacements (false teeth). Fake is also a verb (He faked an injury) and a noun (The painting is a fake).

Fake has 2 distinct senses.

1. Made to look like something real, to trick people or to cost less. (поддельный, искусственный)

  1. The police arrested him for using a fake ID.
  2. Is that fur real or fake?
  3. The site is full of fake reviews.

2. Pretended; not sincere. (притворный, фальшивый)

  1. She gave me a big fake smile.
  2. I can't stand him — he's so fake.
  3. His Southern accent sounds fake.

Opposite

Contrast
False vs fake: a false statement is not true; a fake Rolex is a copy made to fool you. Russian фальшивые деньги are counterfeit or fake money, not false money.

False has 4 distinct senses.

  3. He gave the police a false name.

2. Based on a mistake; wrong. (ошибочный, напрасный)

  1. It was a false alarm — there was no fire.
  2. Don't give her false hope.
  3. I had a false impression of him.

3. Not natural; artificial. (искусственный, накладной)

  1. My grandfather has false teeth.
  2. She wore false eyelashes to the party.

4. Not sincere; pretended. (притворный, фальшивый)

  1. Spare me the false modesty.
  2. Her cheerfulness sounded false.

fast#

Pronunciation
/fæst/ · FAST
Русский
быстрый; спешащий (о часах); верный, прочный
Type
quality
Position
both — in the 'clock' sense only after a verb: My watch is fast; in fast friends only before a noun
Comparison
-er/-est — faster, fastest
Opposite
slow
Contrast
Fast is also the adverb: drive fast, never fastly. Fast is mostly about speed (a fast car); quick is about taking a short time (a quick question), though they often overlap. In fast asleep it means 'firmly', and the verb to fast means to go without food.

Fast has 4 distinct senses.

1. Moving or able to move at high speed. (быстрый, скоростной)

  1. He drives a really fast car.
  2. She's the fastest runner on the team.
  3. The fast lane is on the left.

2. Doing something or happening in a short time. (быстрый, скорый)

  1. He's a fast learner.
  2. The internet here is much faster than at home.
  3. We need a fast decision.

3. Of a clock or watch: showing a time later than the real time. (спешащий)

  1. My watch is five minutes fast.
  2. The clock in the car is fast, so don't panic.

4. Firm and lasting — in fast friends, and of dye that does not wash out. (верный, прочный; нелиняющий)

  1. The two girls became fast friends at camp.
  2. Wash it in cold water — the dye isn't fast.

fatal#

Pronunciation
/ˈfeɪtəl/ · FAY-tuhl

Position

Comparison

Contrast
Russian фатальный often means 'fateful' or 'inevitable'; English fatal means 'causing death' or 'causing ruin'. A fatal accident is one in which someone died. For 'deciding the future', use fateful.

Fatal has 2 distinct senses.

  1. There was a fatal accident on the highway last night.

  1. Trusting him was a fatal mistake.
  2. The plan has one fatal flaw.

fatalistic#

Pronunciation
/ˌfeɪtəˈlɪstɪk/ · fay-tuh-LIS-tik
Русский
фаталистический, фаталистичный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more fatalistic, most fatalistic
Takes
about + noun
Form
-istic from fatalism

Believing that events are decided by fate and cannot be changed.

  1. He's fatalistic about his health — he says what will be will be.
  2. She has a very fatalistic view of politics.
  3. Older voters were more fatalistic about the economy.

fated#

Pronunciation
/ˈfeɪtɪd/ · FAY-tid
Русский
обречённый; предначертанный
Type
participial
Position
both — usually after a verb with a to-infinitive: fated to fail
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
to-infinitive
Form
-ed from fate

Certain to happen or to do something, as if fate has decided it.

  1. Their marriage seemed fated to fail from the start.
  2. Do you think we were fated to meet?
  3. The novel tells the story of two fated lovers.

fateful#

Pronunciation
/ˈfeɪtfəl/ · FAYT-fuhl
Русский
роковой, судьбоносный
Type
evaluative
Position
both — usually before a noun: that fateful day
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ful from fate
Contrast
Fateful means having a very important, often bad, effect on the future; fatal means causing death or ruin. A fateful decision changed everything; a fatal decision led to disaster.

Having an important, often bad, effect on the future.

  1. On that fateful morning, she decided to take the train.
  2. It was a fateful decision that changed his life.
  3. The fateful meeting took place in 1962.

Without a father, because he has died or is not part of the child's life.

  1. She grew up fatherless after her dad died in the war.
  2. The charity helps fatherless children.
  3. Millions of kids are growing up fatherless.

Likely to make you gain weight.

fatty#

Pronunciation
/ˈfæti/ · FAT-ee
Русский
жирный; жировой
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — fattier, fattiest
Form
-y from fat
Contrast
Fatty describes food and body tissue; a person is fat or, more politely, overweight. As a noun, fatty is a rude name for a fat person.

Fatty has 2 distinct senses.

1. Containing a lot of fat. (жирный)

  1. Cut down on fatty foods like burgers and fries.
  2. This cut of meat is fattier than chicken breast.
  3. The soup was too fatty for me.

2. Made of fat or relating to fat in the body — in medicine and science. (жировой)

  1. Doctors found fatty deposits in his arteries.
  2. Omega-3 is a type of fatty acid.

evaluative
Position
both

not gradable
Form
-less from fault

Without any mistakes; perfect.

  1. Her English is faultless.

  3. His driving record is faultless.

faulty#

Pronunciation
/ˈfɔːlti/ · FAWL-tee
Русский
неисправный, бракованный; ошибочный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-y from fault

Faulty has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not working correctly, or not made correctly. (неисправный, бракованный)

  1. The fire was caused by faulty wiring.
  2. The company recalled thousands of faulty airbags.
  3. If the charger is faulty, the store will replace it.

2. Of reasoning or information: containing mistakes. (ошибочный)

  1. The study was based on faulty data.
  2. That's faulty logic.

Faustian#

Pronunciation
/ˈfaʊstiən/ · FOW-stee-uhn
Русский
фаустовский
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — a Faustian bargain
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ian from Faust, the man in the legend who sold his soul to the devil
Contrast
Almost always in a Faustian bargain or a Faustian deal: giving up your values or your future for power, money or success now. Always with a capital F.

Giving up something morally important in exchange for power, money or success.

  1. He made a Faustian bargain with the donors who paid for his campaign.
  2. Taking the money was a Faustian deal she later regretted.
  3. The novel explores the Faustian temptations of fame.

faux#

Pronunciation
/foʊ/ · FOH
Русский
искусственный; притворный
Type
quality
Position
attributive only — faux fur
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Pronounced FOH: the x is silent, as in French. In stores faux is the polite word, so faux leather sounds better than fake leather.

Faux has 2 distinct senses.

1. Made to look like a real material; artificial. (искусственный)

  1. She bought a faux fur coat.
  2. The chairs are covered in faux leather.
  3. The kitchen has faux marble countertops.

2. Pretended; not sincere — often humorous. (притворный, показной)

  1. He answered with faux innocence.
  2. She let out a gasp of faux surprise.

favorable#

Pronunciation
/ˈfeɪvərəbəl/ · FAY-vur-uh-buhl
Русский
положительный, благосклонный; благоприятный
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more favorable, most favorable
Takes
to or toward + an idea or person (approving of it); for + what it helps
Form
-able from favor
Opposite

Favorable has 2 distinct senses.

1. Showing approval; positive. (положительный, благосклонный)

  1. The movie got favorable reviews.
  2. Most voters have a favorable opinion of her.
  3. The board was favorable to the idea.

2. Good for someone or something; likely to help success. (благоприятный, выгодный)

  1. Conditions are favorable for the launch.
  2. We got a loan on favorable terms.
  3. Interest rates are more favorable now than last year.

favored#

Pronunciation
/ˈfeɪvərd/ · FAY-vurd
Русский
предпочитаемый, излюбленный; являющийся фаворитом
Type
participial
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more favored, most favored
Takes
to-infinitive in the 'expected to win' sense: favored to win
Form
-ed from favor

Favored has 2 distinct senses.

1. Preferred; liked more than others. (предпочитаемый, излюбленный)

  1. Bikes are the favored way to get around campus.
  2. It's a favored vacation spot for wealthy New Yorkers.
  3. This was the most favored option in the survey.

2. Expected to win. (являющийся фаворитом)

  1. The Lakers are heavily favored to win tonight.
  2. She's the favored candidate in the race.

favorite#

Pronunciation
/ˈfeɪvərɪt/ · FAY-vur-it
Русский
любимый
Type
evaluative
Position
attributive only — my favorite song; after a verb it is the noun: This one is my favorite
Comparison
not gradable — most favorite is heard but avoided in careful English
Contrast
Favorite already means 'liked most', so my most favorite is redundant. In sports, the favorite (a noun) is the team or player expected to win.

Liked more than all others of the same kind.

  1. What's your favorite movie?
  2. Pizza is my favorite food.
  3. We went back to our favorite restaurant for our anniversary.

Contrast

Praising someone too much in order to win their approval.

  1. The singer was surrounded by fawning fans.
  2. The article was a fawning profile of the CEO.
  3. Critics said the interview was far too fawning.

Worried, confused or upset by something — usually with not.

federal#

Pronunciation
/ˈfɛdərəl/ · FED-ur-uhl
Русский
федеральный; федеративный
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — a federal agency
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
In the US, federal means the national government in Washington, as opposed to the state governments: federal taxes vs state taxes, a federal crime. Russian федеративное государство is a federal state.

Federal has 2 distinct senses.

1. Of the US national government, not the government of a state. (федеральный)

  1. Kidnapping is a federal crime.
  2. He works for a federal agency in Washington.
  3. Federal taxes are due in April.

2. Of a country or system: made of states or regions that share power with a central government. (федеративный)

  1. The United States and Germany both have federal systems of government.
  2. Canada is a federal country with ten provinces.

Feeble has 2 distinct senses.

1. Physically very weak. (слабый, немощный)

  1. Her grandmother was too feeble to walk alone.

2. Not effective or convincing. (жалкий, неубедительный)

  1. That's a feeble excuse.
  2. The team made a feeble attempt to come back.
  3. That was the feeblest joke I've ever heard.

Русский

Contrast
A feline is also a noun, meaning a cat.

Feline has 2 distinct senses.

  1. Feline diseases spread quickly in shelters.
  2. Lions and tigers are feline predators.

2. Like a cat, especially graceful, quiet or sly. (кошачий, грациозный)

female#

Pronunciation
/ˈfiːmeɪl/ · FEE-mayl
Русский
женский; женского пола
Type
classifying
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Opposite
male
Contrast
Female is about sex, used for animals, forms and statistics (female workers, a female cat); feminine describes qualities traditionally linked to women. As a noun, a female for a woman sounds clinical or rude, so say a woman.

Female has 2 distinct senses.

1. Belonging to the sex that can have babies or lay eggs. (женский, женского пола)

  1. Only female mosquitoes bite.
  2. The company has only two female executives.
  3. Is your kitten male or female?

2. Of a plug or part: with a hole that another part fits into. (с гнездом, «мама» (о разъёме))

  1. You need a cable with a female connector on one end.
  2. Screw the pipe into the female fitting.

feminine#

Pronunciation
/ˈfɛmənɪn/ · FEM-uh-nin
Русский
женственный; женского рода
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more feminine, most feminine
Opposite

Feminine has 2 distinct senses.

1. Having qualities traditionally thought typical of women. (женственный)

  1. She likes soft, feminine colors like pink and lavender.
  2. The dress is very feminine.
  3. Her new haircut looks more feminine.

2. In grammar: belonging to the gender class that includes most words for women. (женского рода)

  1. In Spanish, casa is a feminine noun.
  2. Russian adjectives change their endings to agree with feminine nouns.

feral#

Русский

Feral has 2 distinct senses.

1. Of an animal: living wild after being tame, or born to such animals. (одичавший, бродячий)

  1. The neighborhood has a lot of feral cats.
  2. Feral hogs are destroying farms in Texas.

2. Wild and fierce, like a wild animal. (дикий, звериный)

  1. The crowd let out a feral roar.
  2. After a week in the woods, the boys looked feral.

Ferocious has 2 distinct senses.

1. Very fierce and violent. (свирепый)

  1. The guard dog looked ferocious.
  2. Bears can be ferocious when they protect their cubs.

2. Very strong or intense. (яростный, жестокий, сильнейший)

  1. The ferocious heat kept everyone inside.

fertile#

Pronunciation
/ˈfɜːrtəl/ · FUR-tuhl
Русский
плодородный; способный иметь детей; богатый (о воображении)
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more fertile, most fertile
Opposite
infertile; barren (land)
Contrast
American English says FUR-tuhl; British English says FUR-tyl.

Fertile has 3 distinct senses.

1. Of land or soil: able to produce good crops. (плодородный)

  1. The valley has rich, fertile soil.
  2. The Midwest has some of the most fertile farmland in the world.

2. Of people or animals: able to have babies. (способный иметь детей, фертильный)

  1. Doctors ran tests to see if he was fertile.
  2. Women are most fertile a few days each month.

3. Of the mind or a situation: producing many ideas or good results. (богатый; благодатный)

  1. The writer has a fertile imagination.
  2. The city was fertile ground for new bands.

Feverish has 2 distinct senses.

fiber-optic#

Pronunciation
/ˌfaɪbər ˈɑːptɪk/ · fy-bur-AHP-tik
Русский
оптоволоконный
Type
compound; also classifying
Position
attributive only
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — fiber + optic

Using thin glass threads that carry information as light.

  1. Our town finally got fiber-optic internet.
  2. Workers were laying fiber-optic cable along the highway.
  3. Fiber-optic lines carry data much faster than copper wires.

Made of or full of fibers.

  3. Older asparagus stalks are more fibrous and harder to chew.

fickle#

Pronunciation
/ˈfɪkəl/ · FIK-uhl
Русский
непостоянный, переменчивый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more fickle, most fickle

Fickle has 2 distinct senses.

1. Often changing what one likes or who one is loyal to. (непостоянный, ветреный)

  1. Voters can be fickle.
  2. He's a fickle friend who disappears when you need him.

2. Of weather, luck or fame, changing suddenly and unpredictably. (переменчивый)

  1. The weather in April is fickle.
  2. Fame is the most fickle thing in show business.

fictional#

Pronunciation
/ˈfɪkʃənəl/ · FIK-shuh-nuhl
Русский
вымышленный, художественный
Type
classifying
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-al from fiction
Opposite
real
Contrast
Fictional is about stories: a fictional character. Fictitious is about something false, made up to fool people: a fictitious name.

Existing only in a story, not in real life.

  1. Sherlock Holmes is a fictional detective.
  2. The town in the movie is fictional.
  3. All the characters in this book are fictional.

fictitious#

Pronunciation
/fɪkˈtɪʃəs/ · fik-TISH-uhs
Русский
фиктивный, ложный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Fictitious means false and made up, often to deceive: a fictitious address. For characters in a story, fictional is the usual word.

Invented and not true, often to fool people.

  1. He checked into the hotel under a fictitious name.
  2. The company created fictitious invoices to hide the money.
  3. The story he told the police turned out to be completely fictitious.

fiduciary#

Pronunciation

Русский
фидуциарный, доверительный
Type
classifying
Position

Comparison

In law and finance, involving a duty to manage money or property for someone else's benefit.

  1. Financial advisers have a fiduciary duty to act in their clients' best interest.
  2. The board was accused of breaching its fiduciary responsibilities.
  3. The relationship between a trustee and a beneficiary is fiduciary.

fiendish#

Pronunciation
/ˈfiːndɪʃ/ · FEEN-dish
Русский
дьявольский; чертовски трудный
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more fiendish, most fiendish
Form
-ish from fiend

Fiendish has 2 distinct senses.

1. Cruel, evil and cunning. (дьявольский, злодейский)

  1. The villain came up with a fiendish plan to rob the bank.
  2. He gave a fiendish laugh.

2. Extremely difficult or cleverly complicated. (чертовски трудный, заковыристый)

  1. This week's crossword was fiendish.
  2. It's the most fiendish puzzle I've ever tried.

fierce#

Pronunciation
/fɪrs/ · FEERS
Русский
свирепый; ожесточённый, сильный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — fiercer, fiercest

Fierce has 2 distinct senses.

1. Angry, aggressive and ready to attack. (свирепый, яростный)

  1. The dog looked fierce, but it was actually friendly.
  2. The tribe was known for its fierce warriors.

2. Very strong or intense. (ожесточённый, сильный)

  1. Competition for the job was fiercer than we expected.
  2. A fierce storm knocked out power across the state.
  3. She is a fierce defender of free speech.

fiery#

Pronunciation
/ˈfaɪəri/ · FY-uh-ree
Русский
огненный; пылкий, вспыльчивый; жгучий
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more fiery, most fiery
Form
-y from fire

Fiery has 3 distinct senses.

1. Full of fire, or bright red or orange like fire. (огненный)

  1. The plane went down in a fiery crash.
  2. She has fiery red hair.

2. Full of strong emotion, especially anger or passion. (пылкий, вспыльчивый)

  1. The senator gave a fiery speech.
  2. Her brother has an even more fiery temper than she does.

3. Of food, very hot and spicy. (жгучий, острый)

  1. This salsa is fiery, so go easy on it.
  2. He likes his wings with a fiery chili sauce.

Also an adverb: We split the bill fifty-fifty.

Divided equally between two; with an equal chance either way.

  1. There's a fifty-fifty chance of rain tomorrow.
  2. The two brothers have a fifty-fifty partnership.
  3. The odds of winning are about fifty-fifty.

figurative#

Pronunciation
/ˈfɪɡjərətɪv/ · FIG-yur-uh-tiv
Русский
переносный, образный; фигуративный
Type
classifying
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Opposite
literal; in art, abstract

Figurative has 2 distinct senses.

1. Of words, used in a way different from their basic meaning to create an image. (переносный, образный)

  1. 'Break a leg' is a figurative expression.
  2. I didn't mean it literally — it was figurative.
  3. The poem is full of figurative language.

2. Of art, showing people and things as they really look. (фигуративный, изобразительный)

  1. The museum has a large collection of figurative paintings.
  2. Her early work was figurative, but she later turned to abstract art.

fine#

Pronunciation
/faɪn/ · FYN
Русский
нормальный; прекрасный; тонкий; мелкий
Type
evaluative; also quality
Position
both — the 'all right' sense mostly after a verb: I'm fine
Comparison
-er/-est — finer, finest
Contrast
I'm fine means 'all right, nothing wrong', not 'wonderful'. A fine is a noun (штраф: a parking fine), and in speech fine is also an adverb: The printer works fine.

Fine has 5 distinct senses.

1. All right, acceptable, or in good health. (нормальный, в порядке)

  1. "How are you?" "I'm fine, thanks."
  2. Tuesday is fine with me.
  3. Don't worry — the car is fine.

2. Of very high quality. (прекрасный, превосходный)

  1. The restaurant serves fine wines.
  2. This is the finest chocolate in the country.
  3. He did a fine job on the kitchen.

3. Very thin or narrow. (тонкий)

  1. Her hair is very fine and hard to style.
  2. Draw a fine line along the edge with a pencil.

4. Made of very small pieces. (мелкий)

  1. The beach is covered in fine white sand.
  2. Use the finer side of the grater for the cheese.

5. Small and subtle, so hard to notice or understand. (тонкий, едва уловимый)

  1. There's a fine line between confidence and arrogance.
  2. The coach explained the finer points of the game.

fine-grained#

Pronunciation
/ˌfaɪn ˈɡreɪnd/ · fyn-GRAYND
Русский
мелкозернистый; детальный
Type
compound; also quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more fine-grained, most fine-grained
Form
compound — fine + grained

Fine-grained has 2 distinct senses.

1. Of wood, stone or other material, having a smooth texture made of very small grains. (мелкозернистый)

  1. Maple is a hard, fine-grained wood.
  2. The sculptor chose a fine-grained white marble.

2. Very detailed, dealing with small parts separately. (детальный, подробный)

  1. The new app gives you more fine-grained control over your privacy settings.
  2. We need a fine-grained analysis of the sales data.

finicky#

Pronunciation
/ˈfɪnɪki/ · FIN-i-kee
Русский
привередливый; капризный, мудрёный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more finicky, most finicky
Takes
about + noun — fussy about something

Finicky has 2 distinct senses.

1. Very hard to please, especially about small details. (привередливый, разборчивый)

  1. My cat is a finicky eater.
  2. He's finicky about how his coffee is made.

2. Of a task or machine, needing a lot of care and attention to work. (капризный, мудрёный)

  1. The old printer is finicky — you have to feed the paper by hand.
  2. This is the most finicky recipe I've ever tried.

finished#

Pronunciation
/ˈfɪnɪʃt/ · FIN-isht
Русский
законченный, готовый; конченый
Type
participial
Position
both — the 'ruined' sense only after a verb
Comparison
not gradable
Takes

Form

Opposite

Finished has 2 distinct senses.

1. Completed, or done with an activity. (законченный, готовый)

  1. Are you finished with the scissors?
  2. The finished house looks amazing.
  3. Once the paint dries, the table will be finished.

2. Ruined, with no hope of success or of continuing. (конченый, погибший)

  1. After the scandal, his political career was finished.
  2. If the bank says no, we're finished.

Having a limit or an end.

fire-resistant#

Pronunciation
/ˌfaɪər rɪˈzɪstənt/ · fy-ur-ri-ZIS-tuhnt
Русский
огнестойкий
Type
compound; also quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more fire-resistant, most fire-resistant
Form
compound — noun + adjective
Contrast
Fire-resistant means hard to burn or slow to burn; fireproof means fire cannot damage it at all.

Not burning easily, or protecting against fire for some time.

  1. Firefighters wear fire-resistant suits.
  2. Brick is more fire-resistant than wood.
  3. The new curtains in the school auditorium are fire-resistant.

fire-retardant#

Pronunciation
/ˌfaɪər rɪˈtɑːrdənt/ · fy-ur-ri-TAR-duhnt
Русский
огнезащитный
Type
compound; also classifying
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — noun + adjective
Contrast
Same meaning as flame-retardant. Without the hyphen it is often a noun: Planes dropped fire retardant on the hills.

Treated with or made of a substance that slows the spread of fire.

  1. The theater curtains are fire-retardant.
  2. Workers sprayed a fire-retardant coating on the wooden beams.
  3. Crews used fire-retardant gel to protect homes near the wildfire.

firm#

Pronunciation
/fɜːrm/ · FURM
Русский
твёрдый; крепкий; строгий
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — firmer, firmest
Takes
with + person — strict with someone
Contrast
A firm is also a noun — a company: a law firm.

Firm has 4 distinct senses.

1. Fairly hard and not soft when pressed. (твёрдый, упругий)

  1. I sleep better on a firm mattress.
  2. Bake the cake until the top is firm.
  3. Choose the firmest peaches for the pie.

2. Strong and steady. (крепкий)

  1. He has a firm handshake.
  2. Keep a firm grip on the rope.

3. Strict and showing that you are in control. (строгий, твёрдый)

  1. You need to be firm with the kids about bedtime.
  2. She's firm but fair with her students.

4. Definite and not likely to change. (твёрдый, окончательный)

  1. We don't have a firm date for the wedding yet.
  2. She's a firm believer in hard work.
  3. Is that a firm offer?

Fishy has 2 distinct senses.

  1. Something fishy is going on here.

2. Tasting or smelling of fish. (рыбный, с рыбным запахом)

  1. The kitchen had a fishy smell.

fixed#

Pronunciation
/fɪkst/ · FIKST
Русский
фиксированный; починенный; неподвижный; подстроенный
Type
participial
Position
both — the 'repaired' sense only after a verb
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ed from fix

Fixed has 4 distinct senses.

1. Not changing; set at a particular amount or level. (фиксированный, постоянный)

  1. We pay a fixed rate on our mortgage.
  2. The prices are fixed, so don't try to bargain.
  3. Retirees on fixed incomes were hit hardest by inflation.

2. Repaired and working again. (починенный)

  1. Is the dishwasher fixed yet?
  2. Once the brakes are fixed, you can drive it again.

3. Attached in one place so that it cannot move. (закреплённый, неподвижный)

  1. The camera stays in a fixed position.
  2. The benches in the park are fixed, so you can't move them.

4. Informal: arranged dishonestly so the result is decided in advance. (подстроенный)

  1. Fans claimed the fight was fixed.
  2. Nobody believed it was anything but a fixed election.

flabbergasted#

flabby#

Pronunciation
/ˈflæbi/ · FLAB-ee
Русский
дряблый, обрюзгший; вялый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — flabbier, flabbiest
Form
-y from flab

Flabby has 2 distinct senses.

1. Of the body, soft and loose, with too much fat. (дряблый, обрюзгший)

  1. He's gotten flabby since he stopped running.
  2. My stomach is flabbier than it used to be.
  3. She does push-ups to tone her flabby arms.

2. Weak and not effective, with too much that is not needed. (вялый, рыхлый)

  1. The essay is flabby and needs serious cutting.
  2. The movie's flabby middle hour really drags.

flaccid#

Pronunciation
/ˈflæsɪd/ · FLAS-id
Русский
вялый, дряблый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more flaccid, most flaccid
Contrast
Two pronunciations are heard in American English: FLAS-id /ˈflæsɪd/ and FLAK-sid /ˈflæksɪd/.

Soft, weak and hanging loosely; also, lacking force or energy.

  1. The plants were flaccid from lack of water.
  2. After a week of rest, his muscles felt flaccid.
  3. Critics called the sequel even more flaccid than the original.

flagging#

Pronunciation
/ˈflæɡɪŋ/ · FLAG-ing
Русский
ослабевающий, падающий
Type
participial
Position
attributive only
Comparison
not gradable
Form

Contrast
Nothing to do with a flag (флаг). After be it is the verb: Our energy was flagging.

Becoming weaker, less active or less successful.

Usually a verb or noun: flush the toilet (смывать), a flush of anger (прилив).

Flush has 2 distinct senses.

1. Exactly level with another surface. (вровень, заподлицо)

  1. Make sure the shelf is flush with the wall.
  2. The new cabinets are flush against the ceiling.

2. Informal: having a lot of money. (при деньгах)

  1. After selling the house, they were flush with cash.
  2. Tech companies were flush that year and hiring fast.

Flushed has 2 distinct senses.

  1. Flushed with victory, the team celebrated all night.
  2. The candidate, flushed with success, thanked her supporters.

foreign#

Pronunciation
/ˈfɔːrən/ · FOR-uhn
Русский
иностранный, зарубежный; внешний; чуждый
Type
origin; also classifying
Position
both — the 'other countries' and 'foreign object' senses only before a noun
Comparison
not gradable — the 'unfamiliar' sense takes more/most: more foreign
Takes
to + person, in the 'unfamiliar' sense
Opposite
domestic
Contrast
Use foreign for things: foreign cars, foreign languages. For people, foreigner can sound unfriendly; Americans often say international students or visitors from abroad instead.

Foreign has 4 distinct senses.

1. From, in or typical of a country that is not your own. (иностранный, зарубежный)

  1. She speaks three foreign languages.
  2. Most of the cars in the lot were foreign.
  3. The city attracts millions of foreign tourists every year.

2. Dealing with or involving other countries. (внешний (о политике))

  1. The president's foreign policy was unpopular.
  2. She reports on foreign affairs for a Chicago paper.

3. Strange and unfamiliar to someone. (чуждый, незнакомый)

  1. The idea of saving money was completely foreign to him.
  2. Life on a farm felt foreign at first.

4. Coming from outside and not belonging where it is found. (инородный)

  1. The X-ray showed a foreign object in his stomach.
  2. The doctor removed a foreign body from the child's ear.

formal#

Pronunciation
/ˈfɔːrməl/ · FOR-muhl
Русский
официальный; строгий (о стиле)
Type
quality
Position
both — the 'official' and 'education' senses usually before a noun
Comparison
more/most — more formal, most formal
Opposite
informal
Contrast
False friend. Russian формальный подход (done only for show) is perfunctory or box-ticking in English, not formal. English formal means serious and official in style.

Formal has 3 distinct senses.

1. Serious and correct in style, following the rules for official occasions, clothes or language. (официальный, строгий, торжественный)

  1. The wedding invitation says formal attire.
  2. Use formal language in a cover letter.
  3. He's more formal than his brother and always calls me Ms. Chen.

2. Official and done according to the proper rules. (официальный)

  1. She filed a formal complaint with HR.
  2. We haven't received a formal offer yet.
  3. There was no formal agreement between them.

3. Received through schools, colleges and training programs. (систематическое, полученное в учебном заведении (об образовании))

  1. He had little formal education but read constantly.
  2. She has no formal training in design.

  3. He has a formidable memory for names.

2. Very difficult to deal with. (труднейший, серьёзный)

  1. Rebuilding the town is a formidable task.
  2. The mountain presents formidable challenges for climbers.

forthcoming#

Pronunciation
/ˌfɔːrθˈkʌmɪŋ/ · forth-KUHM-ing
Русский
предстоящий; откровенный; предоставляемый
Type
quality
Position
both — the 'coming soon' sense before a noun, the other two after a verb
Comparison
not gradable — the 'willing to talk' sense takes more/most: more forthcoming
Form
forth + coming
Contrast
The meaning changes with position: a forthcoming report will appear soon; he wasn't forthcoming means he didn't say much; no help was forthcoming means none was given.

Forthcoming has 3 distinct senses.

1. Going to happen or appear soon. (предстоящий, готовящийся к выходу)

  1. Her forthcoming book is about the Civil War.
  2. Details will be announced in a forthcoming report.

2. Willing to give information or talk openly. (откровенный, открытый)

  1. He wasn't very forthcoming about his past.
  2. The company has been more forthcoming since the lawsuit.

3. Given or provided when needed; usually in negative sentences. (предоставляемый, поступающий)

  1. No explanation was forthcoming.
  2. If funding isn't forthcoming, the project will stop.

forward#

Pronunciation
/ˈfɔːrwərd/ · FOR-wurd
Русский
направленный вперёд; передний; развязный
Type
classifying; also quality
Position

Comparison
not gradable — the 'too bold' sense takes more/most: more forward
Opposite
backward
Contrast
Usually an adverb: step forward, look forward to. The adjective is the one before a noun (forward motion) or the 'too bold' sense (a bit forward).

Forward has 3 distinct senses.

1. Moving or directed toward the front or ahead. (направленный вперёд, поступательный)

  1. The team made little forward progress in the second half.
  2. Lean your body into a slightly forward position.
  3. A forward pass is legal in football but not in rugby.

2. At or near the front of a ship, plane or vehicle. (передний, носовой)

  1. Passengers in the forward cabin get off first.
  2. The forward seats have more legroom.

3. Too confident or friendly with someone you do not know well. (развязный, нахальный)

  1. It was a little forward of him to ask for her number so soon.
  2. I hope I'm not being too forward.
  3. She was more forward than the other girls in class.

Pronunciation

Русский

Type

Position

Comparison

Contrast
Also a noun and a verb in sports: commit a foul, he fouled out. Sounds the same as fowl (birds such as chickens).

Foul has 4 distinct senses.

1. Very unpleasant to smell or taste. (зловонный, мерзкий)

  1. A foul smell was coming from the fridge.
  2. The water tasted foul.
  3. It was the foulest coffee I've ever had.

2. Very bad or unpleasant, of weather, a mood or a temper. (скверный, ужасный)

  1. He's been in a foul mood all day.
  2. Foul weather delayed the launch.

3. Rude and offensive, full of swearing. (грубый, непристойный)

  1. The coach was fined for foul language.
  2. She has a foul mouth when she's angry.

4. Against the rules or the law; in baseball, hit outside the lines. (нечестный, противоправный; вне поля (в бейсболе))

  1. The umpire called it a foul ball.
  2. Police suspect foul play in his death.

Pronunciation
/frɛʃ/ · FRESH
Русский
свежий; новый; пресный; бодрый
Type
quality
Position
both — the 'just come from' and 'rude' senses after a verb
Comparison
-er/-est — fresher, freshest
Takes
from or out of + noun, in the 'just come from' sense
Opposite
stale (bread); frozen, canned (food)

Fresh has 8 distinct senses.

1. Recently made, picked or caught; not old, frozen or canned. (свежий)

  1. We bought fresh corn at the farmers market.
  2. Is this bread fresh?
  3. The freshest fish comes in on Tuesday mornings.

2. New or different, replacing something old or used. (новый, другой)

  1. After the divorce she wanted a fresh start.
  2. The team needs some fresh ideas.
  3. Let me get you a fresh towel.

3. Recent, just done, or still clear in the mind. (свежий, недавний)

  1. The memory is still fresh in my mind.
  2. Watch out for the fresh paint!
  3. There was a foot of fresh snow on the ground.

4. Of air or a smell: clean, cool and pleasant. (свежий, чистый)

  1. Let's go outside and get some fresh air.
  2. The sheets had a fresh, clean smell.

5. Of water: not salty. (пресный)

  1. The island has no source of fresh water.
  2. These fish can't survive in fresh water.

6. Full of energy; not tired. (бодрый, отдохнувший)

  1. I felt fresh after a good night's sleep.
  2. She looked fresher after a shower and a coffee.

7. Having just come from a place, school or experience. (только что (окончивший, прибывший))

  1. He's fresh out of college.
  2. The chef came to us fresh from a job in Paris.

8. Informal and a little old-fashioned: rude or disrespectful, often in a sexual way. (дерзкий, нахальный)

  1. Don't get fresh with me, young man.
  2. He got fresh with the waitress and was asked to leave.

freshman#

Pronunciation
/ˈfrɛʃmən/ · FRESH-muhn
Русский
первокурсный; находящийся на первом сроке
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
More often a noun (She is a freshman). As an adjective it stands only before a noun: freshman year, never the year is freshman.

Freshman has 2 distinct senses.

1. Of the first year of high school or college. (первокурсный, для первого года обучения)

  1. Freshman year was the hardest of the four.
  2. He teaches a freshman composition class.

2. Serving a first term in office. (впервые избранный (о политике))

  1. The bill was written by a freshman senator.
  2. Freshman lawmakers get the worst offices in the building.

Freudian#

Pronunciation
/ˈfrɔɪdiən/ · FROY-dee-uhn
Русский
фрейдистский
Type
classifying
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable — informally very Freudian
Form
Freud + -ian

Freudian has 2 distinct senses.

1. Relating to Sigmund Freud and his theories of the mind. (фрейдистский)

  1. The novel is full of Freudian symbols.
  2. Her therapist takes a Freudian approach.

2. Showing hidden, often sexual, thoughts or feelings — especially in a Freudian slip. (фрейдистский (об оговорке))

  1. Calling my boss 'Mom' was a Freudian slip.
  2. There's something very Freudian about his fear of snakes.

frictionless#

Pronunciation
/ˈfrɪkʃənləs/ · FRIK-shuhn-luhs
Русский
без трения; беспрепятственный
Type
classifying
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more frictionless, most frictionless
Form
-less from friction

Frictionless has 2 distinct senses.

1. In physics, having no friction. (без трения)

  1. Imagine a ball rolling on a frictionless surface.
  2. No real machine is completely frictionless.

2. Smooth and easy, with no delays or difficulties. (беспрепятственный, лёгкий)

  1. The app promises frictionless payments.
  2. Online shopping has become almost frictionless.
  3. The company wants a more frictionless checkout.

fried#

Pronunciation
/fraɪd/ · FRYD
Русский
жареный; вымотанный; сгоревший
Type
participial
Position
both — the informal senses after a verb
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ed from fry, with y changed to i
Contrast
fried means cooked in oil or fat. Russian жареный also covers roasting and grilling: meat cooked in the oven is roast, on a grill grilled. Food cooked covered in hot oil is deep-fried.

Fried has 3 distinct senses.

1. Cooked in hot oil or fat. (жареный)

  1. She ordered fried chicken and mashed potatoes.
  2. Do you want your eggs fried or scrambled?
  3. Too much fried food is bad for your heart.

2. Informal: very tired and unable to think clearly. (вымотанный, выжатый)

  1. My brain is fried after that exam.
  2. I'm totally fried — I need a break.

3. Informal: of electronics, destroyed by too much electricity or heat. (сгоревший)

  1. The storm left our router fried.
  2. Check whether the motherboard is fried.

Contrast
False friend. Russian фригидный is only about sex; that sense of frigid is old-fashioned and offensive in English. Americans most often use frigid about weather and water.

Frigid has 2 distinct senses.

  1. Frigid air moved into the Midwest overnight.
  2. The water in the lake was frigid.
  3. It was the most frigid January on record.

2. Unfriendly and without any warmth. (холодный, ледяной (о манерах))

  1. She gave him a frigid stare.
  2. The meeting ended on a frigid note.

frosted#

Pronunciation

Contrast
American frosting is British icing, so a frosted cake is a cake covered with icing.

Frosted has 3 distinct senses.

  1. The frosted grass crunched under our boots.

  2. Beat the eggs until they're frothy.

frozen#

Pronunciation
/ˈfroʊzən/ · FROH-zuhn
Русский
замёрзший; замороженный; оцепеневший
Type
participial
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-en participle of freeze

Frozen has 5 distinct senses.

1. Turned into ice, or made hard by cold. (замёрзший)

  1. The kids went skating on the frozen pond.
  2. The ground was frozen solid.

2. Of food: kept at a very low temperature to preserve it. (замороженный)

  1. Grab a bag of frozen peas.
  2. Frozen pizza is his idea of dinner.
  3. Is the chicken fresh or frozen?

3. Of a person: very cold. (замёрзший, продрогший)

  1. Come inside — you must be frozen!
  2. My feet are frozen.

4. Unable to move because of fear or shock. (оцепеневший, застывший)

  1. She stood frozen in the doorway.
  2. He was frozen with fear.

5. Of money, prices or a screen: fixed and not able to change or move. (замороженный (о счетах, ценах); зависший (об экране))

  1. Their bank accounts are still frozen.
  2. My screen is frozen again.

fucked-up#

Pronunciation

Русский

Form
