Contrast
Vulgar slang and highly offensive — avoid it at work and with people you don't know. The polite version is messed-up.

Fucked-up has 3 distinct senses.

3. Vulgar slang: very drunk or high on drugs. (обдолбанный, в хлам)

  1. Everyone at the party was completely fucked up.
  2. He got fucked up on cheap vodka.

  2. Full-strength coffee keeps me up all night.
  3. The cleaner is too harsh if you use it full-strength.

full-term#

Born or carried for the whole normal length of a pregnancy.

fulsome#

Pronunciation
/ˈfʊlsəm/ · FUUL-suhm
Русский
неумеренный, приторный (о похвале)
Type
evaluative
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
more/most — more fulsome, most fulsome
Contrast
Traditionally an insult: fulsome praise is so thick that it sounds fake. Many Americans now use it simply to mean full or thorough, and careful readers still object.

Fulsome has 2 distinct senses.

1. So excessive that it sounds insincere. (чрезмерный, приторный)

  1. He thanked the host in fulsome terms.
  2. The review's fulsome praise made me suspicious.

2. Full and generous — a newer use, common in the press. (полный, исчерпывающий)

  1. The senator promised a fulsome response to the questions.
  2. Reporters asked for a more fulsome account of the meeting.

fun#

Pronunciation
/fʌn/ · FUHN
Русский
весёлый, приятный
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more fun, most fun
Contrast
fun means you enjoy it; funny means it makes you laugh. a fun movie is entertaining, a funny movie is a comedy. fun is also a noun (We had a lot of fun), and funner/funnest are children's forms.

Enjoyable; giving pleasure.

  1. We had a fun weekend at the lake.
  2. She's a fun person to work with.
  3. Skiing turned out to be more fun than I expected.

functional#

Pronunciation
/ˈfʌŋkʃənəl/ · FUHNGK-shuh-nuhl
Русский
работающий, исправный; практичный; функциональный
Type
quality; also classifying
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more functional, most functional
Form
-al from function
Opposite
nonfunctional
Contrast
A dysfunctional family is not one that fails to work in this sense — dysfunctional means unhealthy relationships.

Functional has 3 distinct senses.

1. Working, and able to be used. (работающий, исправный)

  1. Only one of the elevators is functional.
  2. The kitchen is small but fully functional.

2. Designed to be practical rather than attractive. (практичный, функциональный)

  1. The furniture is plain and functional.
  2. It's a functional building, not a beautiful one.
  3. The new layout is more functional than the old one.

3. Relating to the way something works. (функциональный)

  1. There's no functional difference between the two models.
  2. The scan showed no functional damage.

functionally illiterate#

Pronunciation
/ˈfʌŋkʃənəli ɪˈlɪtərət/ · FUNGK-shuh-nuh-lee i-LIT-ur-it

Type

Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
adverb + adjective

Able to read a little, but not well enough to handle everyday forms, signs and instructions.

  1. About a tenth of the adults tested were functionally illiterate.
  2. He hid for years that he was functionally illiterate.
  3. The program helps functionally illiterate adults fill out job forms.

Working as it should.

  3. A fully functioning immune system clears the virus in days.

Forming the base of something, and therefore the most important.

  3. The problem is more fundamental than a software bug.

fungible#

A law and finance word, familiar to most people from non-fungible token.

Able to be exchanged for another unit of exactly the same kind.

funky#

Pronunciation
/ˈfʌŋki/ · FUHNG-kee
Русский
необычный, стильный; фанковый; с душком
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — funkier, funkiest

Funky has 3 distinct senses.

1. Unusual and stylish in a relaxed, offbeat way. (необычный, стильный)

  1. She wears funky glasses.
  2. It's a funky little coffee shop on Third.
  3. The place got funkier after the new owners took over.

2. Of music: with a strong, danceable rhythm. (фанковый, ритмичный)

  1. The band has a funky bass line.
  2. I love the funky stuff they recorded in the seventies.

3. Smelling bad. (с душком, вонючий)

  1. Something in the fridge smells funky.
  2. His gym bag is seriously funky.

furrowed#

Pronunciation

Furrowed has 2 distinct senses.

1. Marked with deep lines or wrinkles. (изборождённый морщинами, нахмуренный)

2. Cut into long grooves, as a plowed field is. (вспаханный бороздами)

  1. We walked across the furrowed field.
  2. The furrowed ground was ready for seed.

fussy#

Pronunciation
/ˈfʌsi/ · FUHS-ee
Русский
привередливый; капризный; вычурный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — fussier, fussiest
Takes
about + noun or -ing
Contrast
picky is the everyday American word for the first sense: a picky eater.

Fussy has 3 distinct senses.

1. Hard to please, and worried about small details. (привередливый)

  1. He's fussy about what he eats.
  2. Don't be so fussy — it's just a sandwich.
  3. She's fussier than her sister about grammar.

2. Of a baby: crying and unsettled. (капризный, беспокойный)

  1. The baby's fussy tonight.
  2. He gets fussy around bedtime.

3. Of a design: crowded with too many small details. (вычурный, аляповатый)

  1. The wallpaper is a little fussy for my taste.
  2. The dress is pretty, but the lace is fussy.

fuzzy#

Pronunciation
/ˈfʌzi/ · FUHZ-ee
Русский
пушистый; размытый, нечёткий
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — fuzzier, fuzziest
Contrast
warm and fuzzy is a fixed phrase for a comfortable, sentimental feeling: The ad gave me a warm and fuzzy feeling.

Fuzzy has 2 distinct senses.

1. Covered with short soft hairs. (пушистый)

  1. He gave her a fuzzy teddy bear.
  2. The peaches were warm and fuzzy.

2. Not clear or sharp, whether a picture, a memory or a rule. (размытый, нечёткий)

  1. The photo came out fuzzy.
  2. My memory of that night is fuzzy.
  3. The rules get fuzzier the more you read them.
